# Non-Aiken test quality audit

**Date:** 2026-09-09
**Rubric:** `docs/research/testing-best-practices.md` (15 sections plus the review gate)
**Scope:** every non-Aiken test file in the main checkout: 898 vitest / node:test files under `demo/` plus the 12 Haskell modules under `offchain/tests` and `onchain/plutarch/tests`. Aiken tests are deferred to a later pass.
**Method:** each file was read in full by one of 34 reviewers working from a shared rubric, opening the production module or helper it depends on wherever the verdict turned on oracle provenance. Nothing was executed; every "red today" claim below was re-checked by hashing the built blueprint and grepping the committed ledgers. Every file has one row in the ranked table (`test-quality-audit-2026-09-09.ranked.tsv`, same directory).

## 1. Headline

| Grade | Files | Lines | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| A (9–10) | 325 | 203,993 | credible evidence against a meaningful failure |
| B (7–8) | 409 | 160,767 | sound, with one or two removable weaknesses |
| C (5–6) | 122 | 30,522 | protects something, but the oracle or gate is compromised |
| D (3–4) | 41 | 10,845 | low quality: mostly transcription, stubs, or source greps |
| F (0–2) | 13 | 2,972 | zero-information or actively misleading |

- **735 of 910 files (81%) are A or B.** The suite's centre of gravity is real: real Lucid emulator with the real compiled Aiken validators and local UPLC evaluation, real Postgres shards, real libp2p over loopback, real SQLite and filesystem journals, real CML as an independent oracle, and paired accept/reject cases. That must not be "cleaned up".
- **156 files are brittle** (a behaviour-preserving production change fails them). 112 of those carry a `self-pin`: a hand-maintained copy of the code's own current output. That is the single dominant defect and it is exactly the pattern the owner ruled out on 2026-09-05.
- **54 files are D or F.** 33 of them belong to one template family (`*fit-ledger.test.ts` and its cousins), so the fix is one template change, not 54 edits.
- **Recommendations:** 640 keep, 198 strengthen, 25 derive-oracle, 23 rewrite, 11 wire-into-ci, 7 merge-into, 6 delete.

Per package:

| Package | Files | Lines | A | B | C | D | F |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| midgard-core | 50 | 16,806 | 35 | 11 | 4 | 0 | 0 |
| midgard-watcher | 77 | 69,308 | 39 | 32 | 5 | 1 | 0 |
| lucid-midgard | 21 | 6,688 | 10 | 6 | 4 | 1 | 0 |

| midgard-node-tools | 26 | 13,164 | 6 | 15 | 4 | 1 | 0 |
| demo/scripts | 1 | 246 | 1 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| offchain (Haskell) | 5 | 362 | 0 | 0 | 4 | 1 | 0 |
| onchain/plutarch (Haskell) | 7 | 2,413 | 0 | 3 | 1 | 2 | 1 |

midgard-core and midgard-watcher are the healthiest trees. midgard-fault-proofs holds 35 of the 54 low-quality files and 109 of the 156 brittle ones, almost all in three copy-pasted template families.

## 2. How the ranking works

Score 0–10 per file, on the rubric's own terms: does a passing result constitute credible evidence against a meaningful failure? Grade bands: A 9–10, B 7–8, C 5–6, D 3–4, F 0–2. Ties break on file length (longer first, since more is at stake). Test count, assertion count and coverage were not scored. A 40-line file that pins one external wire vector against a real decoder can be an A; a 1,500-line lifecycle whose refusals all route through a mocked evaluator can be a D.

Every row carries: tags (vocabulary in §3), a one-sentence contract, the concrete defect with line numbers, whether it is brittle, what the brittle assertion actually catches, the by-construction alternative, and a recommendation. Appendix A is the full ranked table; B is every D/F with reasons; C is every C-grade defect; D is every brittle file with its assurance and by-construction replacement.

## 3. Systemic findings

These eight patterns account for nearly every non-A grade. Each one names the defect, what the affected tests still protect, and how to make that protection hold by construction so the test (or the churn) can go away.

### 3.1 Van Rossem fit ledgers: transcribed measurement tables (37 files, 33 of them D/F or brittle C)

**What they do.** `docs/fault-proofs/size-plans/*.json` holds byte/memory/CPU measurements per fault-proof transaction. Each `*-fit-ledger.test.ts` reads that JSON and asserts margins against the Van Rossem limits. Three sub-variants exist:

- *Tautological digest* (11 files: `receive-purpose-language`, `unused-script-witness`, `observers-forbidden-on-untagged-network`, `observer-order-invalid`, `field-item-width-illegal`, `witness-script-decoding`, `resolved-output-non-canonical`, `network-id-wrongful-rejection`, `invalid-range-wrongful-rejection`, `zero-input-wrongful-rejection`, `input-set-uniqueness-wrongful-rejection`). They compare the ledger's `blueprintSha256` to a copy of that same digest typed into the test, or hash the JSON body and compare it to a digest stored in the same JSON. Two static files compared to each other; **cannot fail on any code change**. Three of them import no production module at all.
- *Inline transcribed table* (8 files: `mint-declared-asset-limit`, `missing-redeemer`, `missing-script-source`, `field-preimage-length-mismatch`, `redeemer-canonicity`, `protected-output-signer-missing`, `transaction-output-non-canonical`, `script-integrity-hash-mismatch`). A 14- to 50-row measurement table is transcribed into the test, `buildVanRossemFitLedger` is run over it, and the result is compared to the JSON that the same test writes from the same table under a `MIDGARD_UPDATE_*_LEDGER=1` flag. The `margin > 0` assertions in these files are unreachable-as-a-failure: `entryFromMeasurement` (`src/proof-fit/van-rossem-fit-ledger.ts` L80-89) already throws on a non-positive margin.
- *Derived digest, replayed rows* (18 files, mostly C): they hash `realBlueprintPath` at test time (correct), then re-feed `stored.entries` into the builder and compare to `stored` (self-derivation) and pin hand-maintained row counts (`toHaveLength(241)`, `372`, `119`, `68`) and a literal compiler string `aiken v1.1.23+5adf783`.

**Why low quality / brittle.** A saved measurement is historical evidence, not a new execution (rubric §13). Every blueprint rebuild, fee change or Lucid bump invalidates the rows without any contract violation. The derived-digest variant is at least honest about staleness, and **it is red today**: the built blueprint is `eaf17c21…`, the 26 committed ledgers carry 29 distinct digests, and only 3 match. So `execution-source-script-decoding`, `output-reference-script-decoding`, `script-integrity-hash-missing`, `spend-input-signer-missing`, `inspect-contracts` and `state-queue-yield-publication-admission` should be failing in CI unless something is skipping them. The tautological variant hides the same staleness and stays green.

**What they actually protect.** Two things: (a) a size plan left stale after a rebuild, and (b) a hand-edited JSON. Neither is a production behaviour. The behaviour that matters (every transaction fits the L1 envelope and execution budget) is already asserted **live** by the lifecycle suites that generate the ledgers: `expectProofFit` derives a byte margin against the emulator's real `maxTxSize` plus a 20% execution reserve, and `assertCompleteLifecycleCoverage` fails closed on any missing scenario.

**By construction.** The repo already contains the fix twice over. `tests/support/measured-fit-ledger.ts` (`createMeasuredFitRecorder` / `verifyMeasuredFitLedger`) derives digest and compiler version from the real blueprint and regenerates rows from fresh submissions. `execution-source-script-decoding-lifecycle.test.ts` and `resolved-output-non-canonical-lifecycle.test.ts` measure live and gate margins in the same run. The end state is: the lifecycle suite is the single producer, the ledger JSON becomes a generated artifact with a `--check` mode wired into CI (the repo already runs eight such fixture `:check` generators), and every `*-fit-ledger.test.ts` is deleted. The transcribed tables, digest constants, name lists, row counts and compiler strings go with them.

### 3.2 Hand-maintained roster constants (about 25 files)

Files that transcribe a production list or count into the test and assert it equals itself: all 54 `WORKFLOW_RUNNER_FACTORIES` keys (`workflow-runtime.test.ts` L1101-1156), the 43-entry catalogue id map (`fraud-proof-catalogue-registration.test.ts`), roster counts 524/517/518/54 (`deployment-manifest.test.ts`, `deployment-manifest-identity.test.ts`), a 150-name export list (`api-export-snapshot.test.ts`), ~16 script-title constants (`midgard-contracts.test.ts`), `*_CONFIG_KEYS` and `*_MANIFEST_CONTRACTS` copies across the fault-proof family adapters, the 830-line nested ternary of reference-script keys for all 54 categories (`fault-proof-application.test.ts`).

**What they protect.** An accidental drop, rename or reorder of a registered contract, role or category. That is real, and for deployed category ids it is safety-relevant.

**Why brittle.** Every legitimate append fails them; the in-file comments record five hand re-pins of one manifest id and repeated stale-count incidents.

**By construction.** Assert the invariant, not the roster: every contract has a role and token name, roles are unique, catalogue order is a permutation of the contract set, the id map is append-only against the on-chain catalogue module (a real two-implementation contract). For config-key surfaces, a `satisfies Record<Key, …>` makes an added key a compile error. For the watcher roster, the `transitionTrace` branch already shows the fix: compare against the workflow module's exported reference-contract-name constant. Where a set-equality against a single source of truth already exists in the same file (it usually does), delete the count.

### 3.3 Source text as an oracle (17 files)

`readFileSync` plus `includes`/`indexOf`/regex over production `.ts`, `.mjs`, `.sql`, shell, compose or Aiken source, asserted as proof of runtime behaviour. Worst: `speculative-commit-safety-guard.test.ts` (F, zero production code executed, counts `LOCK TABLE` occurrences), `complete-item-carriage-policy.test.ts` (D, pins identifier occurrence counts of 10 and 2), `min-ada-twin-cross-check.test.ts` (D, a ~250-line bespoke Aiken tokenizer and evaluator inside the test), `migration-runner.test.ts` (D, nine cases assert DDL substrings), `assets.test.mjs` (D, 14 of 20 tests grep shell scripts), and the ~50-assertion block in `l1-control-plane.test.ts` L1422-1556.

**What they protect.** Genuine hazards nothing else covers: acquiring the L1 permit inside a barrier, publishing inside the permit, sign-and-submit before journaling, a scheduler regressing to `await sleep`, a chunked emitter added without a necessity artifact, an HTTP DA route pasted into the router.

**Why brittle.** Any rename, reflow, comment or helper extraction fails them; conversely a behaviour change that keeps the tokens in place passes.

**By construction.** Three routes, all already used elsewhere in the repo: (a) make the wrong order unrepresentable with a token type (`preparePendingSubmission` returns a value `signAndSubmitProgram` requires; a typed preflight result required by `fetchDaAttestationReferenceScripts`); (b) assert at runtime with the semaphore probe the first 1,400 lines of `l1-control-plane.test.ts` already use, or against the per-worker Postgres shard the node config already provisions; (c) move architecture rules to ESLint `no-restricted-imports` / `no-restricted-syntax` and report them as lint. For the Aiken twin cross-check, emit the Aiken-side floor as a generated golden (the `cek-core-step-v1` pattern) so `aiken check` executes the twin instead of a regex parsing it.

### 3.4 Golden vectors without a machine channel (about 20 files, mostly B)

Hex constants annotated "the Aiken vector" with no generator, fixture, or `--check` tying them to the Aiken side: about 60 pinned control encodings and leaf hashes across the midgard-core CEK/ledger/validation-trace/script-proof suites, plus `transition-trace-output-summary`, `ledger-output-commitment`, `input-resolution-schedule-boundary` (~30 digests), the three `nested-*-boundary` files (where `MIDGARD_PRINT_AIKEN_VECTOR=1` also **disables the main assertion**), and `sdk-abi-fixtures.test.ts` whose golden is regenerated by the test itself.

**What they protect.** Wire agreement with the on-chain twin. Where the values were verified to appear verbatim in `*.test.ak` (`script-context-proof`, `cek-constant`, `cek-data`, the ordered-collection family) they are the strongest oracles in the repo and are graded A.

**Why brittle.** With no generator, a legitimate re-encoding re-pins long hex by hand and nothing checks the new value against Aiken; both copies drift together silently.

**By construction.** The repo has the correct template in eight places (`native-tx-field-access-v1`, `native-tx-field-items-v1`, `native-tx-vector-v1`, `native-tx-carriage-wire-v1`, `canonical-decodability-v1`, `committed-field-shape-v1`, `ordered-collection-boundary-aiken`, `cek-core-step-v1`): a generator emits the fixture and the Aiken golden, CI runs `--check`. Moving each family onto that channel is one mechanical change per family. Two channels are missing their CI `--check` today: `fixtures:transaction-root-v1:check` and `validation-auxiliary-witness-v1`.

### 3.5 Stubbed decisions and fictional systems (about 15 files)

`vi.mock` replacing the very module whose decision is claimed: `historical-native-script-preimage.test.ts` (both authority modules mocked; two assertions verify the mock's own WeakSet), `script-integrity-hash-mismatch-manifest-workflow.test.ts` (asserts the exact order of 19 mocked calls), `script-integrity-hash-mismatch-replay.test.ts`, `field-preimage-length-mismatch-config.test.ts`. Hand-written fakes of external systems with no conformance check: the SQL-text-dispatching `SqlClient` in `pipeline-status-route.test.ts`, three copies of a recording Lucid in midgard-sdk, `MemoryBackend` in the watcher (no CAS conformance against the real SQLite backend), `public-retained-da-store.test.ts` (the fake implements the DELETE rejection it then asserts), `ScriptContextBuilder.hs` (a semantic ledger fake every Plutarch test rests on).

**By construction.** Use the real dependency where it is already available: the node package provisions Postgres shards, midgard-sdk already drives the Lucid Emulator in `field-preimage-publication-funding.test.ts`, the watcher has a real SQLite backend. For remaining fakes, add one conformance test each against the real boundary.

### 3.6 Fail-open gates (about 30 files)

- `describe.runIf(hasFamily)` on nine publication-fit suites: a renamed or dropped validator family makes the suite vanish and CI stays green. Four siblings assert presence instead; copy them.
- `describe.skipIf(!optionB)` / blueprint-presence skips: both `option-b-*-frontier` files, both route-freedom files, `complete-item-route-adversarial-emulator` (the only adversarial route matrix), `scratch-cg1-publication-fit` (the 16,384-byte control-validator fit gate), the two `*-forced-*-deployment` files. All documented, all green on a stale or missing blueprint.
- `if (process.env.MIDGARD_WRITE_FIT_LEDGER !== "1") return` inside `afterAll` in about ten lifecycle suites puts the **only acceptance criteria** (13.2M memory / 8G CPU ceilings, `expect(completed).toBe(N)` scenario counts) behind a flag no CI job sets. Those suites spend 15–90 minutes of emulator time and then assert only `txHash.toHaveLength(64)`. Moving the ceilings and counts above the early return costs nothing.
- Opt-in env gates that no workflow sets: `WATCHER_TEST_DATABASE_URL` (the only real-Postgres coverage of `PostgresWatcherStore`, set nowhere in the repo), `MIDGARD_VALIDATION_EVIDENCE` (the 858-second ExUnits ledger and the resolver sweep's only re-derivation lane), `MIDGARD_REAL_BLUEPRINT_PATH` in `fault-proof.test.ts` L1856 (early-returns the 485-line validation-trace parameter-order test and the only 14KB script-size gate, though the in-tree default would work; the sibling file documents fixing this same defect under #609), `WYCHEPROOF_ED25519_OPERATOR`, `PHASE1_*_OPERATOR`, `MIDGARD_RUN_PREPROD_OPERATOR_LIFECYCLE_TESTS`.
- Not in any workflow: `mpf-architecture-g-candidate-summary.test.mjs` (16+ mutation cases), both Haskell suites, `fixtures:transaction-root-v1:check`. `TransactionProofs.hs` is not even imported by `Test.hs`.

### 3.7 Undiscriminating negatives (about 40 files, mostly B)

Bare `.rejects.toThrow()` / `_tag === "Left"` / generic `DaAttestationBuildError` / `rejects.toThrow(/semantic-resolution/)` (a stage label, not a reason): `min-ada-wrongful-rejection-lifecycle` (9), `mpf-commit-candidate-probe-artifacts` (~49 mutation cases), `transition-trace-builder` (9), `phase2-benchmark-report` (12-case loop), the three "exact V1 envelope" parser tables in midgard-node (~40 cases), the two DA attestation files (7). Causality is usually rescued by construction (one-field mutation from a demonstrated-valid base, or a paired positive), which is why these stay B. The repo's own `expectOnchainRefusal` (requires `/failed script execution/`) and `assertAvailabilityRefusal` (requires a UPLC budget failure) are the pattern to spread; adding the refusal code to the parser tables is the cheapest broad improvement in the node package.

### 3.8 Duplicated template families

Eight near-identical `*-central-journal.test.ts` clones over eight copy-pasted `src/*/central-journal.ts` adapters (four pass `testOnlyJournalCategoryAlias: "fieldItemWidthIllegal"`, so the identity they protect is not the one production computes); three `*-authenticated-workflow` copies; three `*-authenticated` copies that open with a runtime assertion over a locally declared array literal; two verbatim `*-evidence-machine` clones; two identical `no-reference-input` / `non-existent-input` fit-ledger and forced-replay pairs; six 240–420 s register-then-activate scenarios in `operator-lifecycle-emulator`. One parameterized suite per family, and deduplicating the production adapters themselves, removes the churn without losing a boundary.

## 4. Low-quality files (D and F), by family

Full per-file reasons with line numbers are in Appendix B. Grouped:

**F (13).** Seven fit-ledger tautologies (§3.1): `mint-declared-asset-limit`, `missing-redeemer`, `missing-script-source`, `field-preimage-length-mismatch`, `redeemer-canonicity`, `protected-output-signer-missing`, `transaction-output-non-canonical`, `network-id-wrongful-rejection`, `input-set-uniqueness-wrongful-rejection`, `zero-input-wrongful-rejection` (recommend: delete six, rewrite the rest onto the live producer). `execution-native-script-invalid.test.ts` asserts an exported constant equals a transcribed copy of itself (delete; enforce at type level). `speculative-commit-safety-guard.test.ts` is 215 lines of source grep with zero production execution (rewrite as a real-Postgres concurrency test plus production invariants). `TransactionProofs.hs` defines no test and is unreachable from `Test.hs` (delete).

**D (41).** Fifteen more fit-ledger / measurement-pin files (§3.1, including both `option-b-direct-frontier-*`, `option-b-reference-frontier`, `resolver-proof-fit-sweep`, `missing-native-script-tx-envelope`, `state-queue-yield-publication-admission`, `transition-trace-output-summary`). Five source-text oracles (§3.3). Four stubbed-decision files (§3.5). Roster transcriptions: `fault-proof-application.test.ts`, `missing-redeemer-registration.test.ts`, `api-export-snapshot.test.ts`, `runtime-capability.test.ts`. Round-trip-only ABI files with no byte pin and no negative: `native-script-family`, `native-inclusion-carriage-abi`, `no-reference-input-carriage`. Dead coverage: `postgres-store.test.ts` (always skips), `operator-lifecycle-preprod.test.ts` (passes when the tx hash is null), `resolver-proof-fit-sweep-generate.test.ts` (a generator misfiled as a test, zero `expect` calls). Haskell: `Operators.hs` and `MembershipValidator.hs` use only `mockchainSucceeds` / `psucceeds`, so an always-accepting validator passes; `Scheduler.hs` has its only negative commented out; `ScriptContextBuilder.hs` is an unverified ledger fake.

## 5. Brittle files: what they buy and how to keep the protection without the test

156 files, listed in full in Appendix D with per-file assurance and replacement. By primary cause:

| Cause | Files | What the brittle assertion actually catches | By-construction replacement |
|---|---|---|---|
| self-pin of current output | 112 | stale size plan; accidental roster drop; unnoticed encoding change | derive at test time; generator with `--check`; assert the relation (`accepted <= limit < adjacent`) not the value |
| measurement replayed, not re-taken | 52 | a hand-edited JSON | live measurement in the producing suite, which already exists |
| implementation coupling (call order, call counts, private shape) | 41 | lease released instead of failed; missing fsync; double query per capture | assert ordering relations only; expose an observable counter; make wrong order a type error |
| tautology | 21 | nothing | delete |
| source-text oracle | 17 | permit-inside-barrier; submit-before-journal; DA route leak | token types, runtime probe, or lint rule |
| external vector with no channel | 15 | TS/Aiken drift | generator + `--check` shared with the Aiken twin |
| duplicated clone | 12 | same as its sibling | one parameterized suite |
| env-gated acceptance criteria | 11 | (only when the flag is set) | hoist the assertion above the early return |
| wall-clock as evidence | 4 | threshold return not waiting for a straggler | explicit barrier the test releases; assert ordering not duration |

Legitimate brittleness worth keeping as-is: the boundary files in midgard-validation (`ordered-collection-*`, `blob-chunk-boundary`) are brittle to a Lucid/CML bump, but the constant they pin is a real ledger boundary, the accepted transaction is really submitted, and the vector feeds a CI-checked Aiken generator. Pinning the signed tx hex would remove even that sensitivity. `mainnet-protocol-parameters.test.ts` is a pin made self-checking by a perturbation test and underwrites the whole emulator family.

## 6. What is exemplary and must not be touched

46 files scored 10. The patterns they share are the ones to copy: real compiled validators on a real emulator with refusals routed through `expectOnchainRefusal`; `assertCompleteLifecycleCoverage` closing every lifecycle fail-closed; the #610 arity guard in `tests/support/emulator/blueprints.ts` that stops an under-applied always-succeeds script from making a whole journey unfalsifiable; differential oracles against CML, noble hashes and the Harmonic UPLC machine; real Postgres MVCC race pinning with a plpgsql trigger (`tx-admissions-claim-load`); real crash injection with a restore-to-green control (`retention-enforcement`, `mpf-native-owner-service`); one-field mutation from a demonstrated-valid base with re-derived digests across the watcher indexers; and the model negative in `submit-init-emulator-value-not-preserved-adversarial` (refuse, then convict honestly on the same thread to prove the refusal was the tamper). The full list is in Appendix A, ranks 1–46.

## 7. Recommended order of work

1. **Fit-ledger family (§3.1):** make the lifecycle suites the single producer via `verifyMeasuredFitLedger`, add a `--check` generator to CI, delete all 37 `*-fit-ledger` / transcribed-frontier tests. This removes 33 of the 54 D/F files and about 70 of the 156 brittle ones in one change, and fixes the red-today digests.
2. **Hoist env-gated acceptance criteria (§3.6):** move the ExUnit ceilings and scenario counts above `MIDGARD_WRITE_FIT_LEDGER` early returns; replace `describe.runIf(hasFamily)` with a presence assertion; delete the vestigial `MIDGARD_REAL_BLUEPRINT_PATH` early return in `fault-proof.test.ts`.
3. **Wire the orphans:** `WATCHER_TEST_DATABASE_URL` in the da-committee-node CI job; `fixtures:transaction-root-v1:check` and `validation-auxiliary-witness-v1` `--check`; `mpf-architecture-g-candidate-summary.test.mjs`; a scheduled job for `test:evidence`; a workflow for the two Haskell suites (or a decision to retire them).
4. **Replace source-text oracles (§3.3)** with the token-type / runtime-probe / lint alternatives listed per file.
5. **Roster constants (§3.2):** delete counts where a set-equality already exists; assert invariants elsewhere.
6. **Golden channels (§3.4):** move the midgard-core CEK/ledger vector families and the `nested-*` boundaries onto the generator + `--check` channel.
7. **Parameterize the clone families (§3.8)** and deduplicate the production central-journal adapters.

## 8. Caveats

- Read-only: no suite was run. "Red today" claims rest on hashing `onchain/aiken/plutus.json` (built 2026-09-08) against the committed ledgers; CI rebuilds the blueprint, so CI redness depends on that build reproducing the local digest (it does, per the 2026-09-01 byte-for-byte check).
- `submit-init-emulator-soundness-honest-operator.test.ts` is deliberately red under #605 with an owner and a do-not-weaken instruction. The fault-proofs package `test` script cannot be green while it is open.
- Reviewers read four very large files by outline plus full test bodies rather than every line (`cek-proof`, `da-transport-vectors`, the two watcher indexer files, `validation-dispute-submit`, `submit-init-emulator-*` at 2,400+ lines); a defect confined to an unread fixture builder could be missed.
- Whether each "Aiken vector" hex actually matches its `.ak` counterpart today was verified only where the reviewer opened the module (validation-01 and a few fault-proofs files). The Aiken pass will close that.
- Grades are one reviewer's judgment per file against a shared rubric; adjacent grades (B/C, C/D) should be read as bands, not ordinals.

## Appendix D. Brittle files: assurance provided and by-construction replacement

| Grade | File | Why brittle and what it actually catches | By-construction alternative |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | `demo/midgard-watcher/tests/verification/phase-a-verifier.test.ts` | yes -- every vocabulary count must be hand-edited whenever a RejectCode is legitimately added; the partition/order/justification checks would still pass with the counts derived. What they catch is a code silently dropped from the published reachable set, narrowing what the verifier claims to cover | Derive the counts (`WATCHER_PHASE_A_CANONICAL_REJECT_CODES.length`) and assert the partition invariants in production code at module load instead; keep only the cross-module check against `Object.values(RejectCodes)` and the evidence-corpus-covers-evidenced-set check as tests |
| A | `demo/midgard-watcher/tests/l1/l1-adapter.test.ts` | L652-L654 pins blockContentDigest to a literal 7686e78a... with no stated contract; any change to the normalized encoding or the CBOR fixture requires hand-editing it. It does protect encoding reproducibility across releases. | Either derive the digest at test time from a checked-in canonical vector file with a `--check` generator, or state the compatibility contract in a comment and move the pin to a versioned wire-vector fixture shared with the consumer. |
| A | `demo/midgard-node/tests/da-multi-peer-integration.test.ts` | L286/L298 pin wall-clock windows (threshold return under 2s, straggler over 10s) with performance.now(); a slow or loaded CI runner fails them with no contract violation. They are also the only evidence that the threshold return does not wait for the straggler, which is the point of the test. | Partial: keep the ordering claim but decouple it from wall clock -- have the slow peer block on an explicit barrier the test releases, and assert that the threshold result resolves before the barrier is released (ordering, not duration). The 10s figure is then the injected delay's own construction, not a measurement. |
| A | `demo/midgard-fault-proofs/tests/submit-init-emulator-removal-lease.test.ts` | Exact event counts (L63-66: 3 loads; 4 renews; 2 awaitTx) would break on a behavior-preserving change to renew cadence; it catches a lease released instead of failed; a missing renew around a long await; and silent state-queue mutation after a mid-flight failure | Ordering could partly be enforced in production by a lease state machine that throws on release-after-failure; the counts themselves have no by-construction substitute -- assert relative ordering only and drop the exact renew count. |
| B | `demo/midgard-validation/tests/validation-machine.test.ts` | Brittle: the two source-text tests and the necessity-consumer map break on a rename, a moved file or a new import with no contract violation. Assurance: the rest of the file is genuinely strong - controlled fail-closed refusals with named messages, exhaustive purpose-kind/redeemer-tag mapping with adjacent-value rejection, and the necessity pin at L3102-3109 correctly derives the live blueprint sha256 and validator hashes rather than copying them. | Replace L420-446 with export/type-level assertions on the auxiliary union; make the scanner-consumer set a build artifact the docs are generated from, or assert only that every consumer has SOME necessity artifact rather than a hand-listed mapping; derive the accumulator digests from an independent hasher. |
| B | `demo/midgard-watcher/tests/verification/block-replay.test.ts` | L521-574 FIXED_TWO_TX_ROOTS and the loose root hexes at L2470/L2473/L2520/L2522/L2611/L2613 are transcribed outputs of the production MPF builder; any change to trie-key encoding or to the shared validation fixtures re-pins all of them. L1264-1266 pins partition cardinalities 13/27/10/50 by hand and L1277-1303 reimplements the reject-code -> forced-validity mapping. What they catch: a silent change to the §5.3 fixed-index out-ref key width / non-minimal 19 0000 index encoding, and a reject code silently migrating between the reachable/phase-A-owned/unclaimed partitions. | Roots: publish the same vectors as a shared cross-implementation fixture consumed by both the TS replay and the Aiken/on-chain side, with a generator + --check in CI, instead of a TS-only transcription. Partition: derive the three sets from one tagged table (code -> owner) and assert only disjointness/totality against RejectCodes -- the counts then need no hand update. The reimplemented mapping at L1277 is a copy of the production branches; a table-driven single source of truth removes it. |
| B | `demo/midgard-fault-proofs/tests/workflow-runtime.test.ts` | The 54-key list breaks on every new family with no contract violation; it catches an accidentally dropped or renamed factory key | Replace L1101-1156 with the existing derivation already in this file: validateWorkflowAdapterCoverage (L1727) enumerates every registered category with its missing-adapter reason -- assert against SDK.FRAUD_PROOF_CATALOGUE_CATEGORY_ORDER instead of a transcribed list. |
| B | `demo/midgard-validation/tests/field-preimage-carriage-fit-emulator.test.ts` | L559-L560, L961-L963, L1025-L1034, L1081-L1105, L1364-L1404 pin exact signed sizes (16648, 269/271/504, 15644, 15148, 15872), min-Ada (2064490, 68231610, 11869740) and datum byte counts (210, 27, 15624, 2547); a protocol-parameter, CML or builder change re-measures every one. What they catch is drift between the §8.10/§8.3-E1 spec tables and the code, and a re-pin of K that does not move the frontier with it (L1090) | The cost model itself is cross-checked against the real signed transaction at L937-L955, which makes the frontier derivable rather than transcribed: derive the frontier by search against the model and assert the relation (lands on maxTxSize, one byte over exceeds it), keeping only the spec-quoted numbers, and generate §8.10's table from the run instead of pinning both halves. Separately, the §8.6 certificate is minted under a stand-in native script (L1499), so nothing here exercises the compiled certificate validator's rules - stated honestly in the file, but the tier-3 claim depends on the Aiken suite |
| B | `demo/midgard-sdk/tests/availability-challenge.test.ts` | A dependency bump that changes CBOR overhead by one byte fails it with no contract violation; what it catches is a change to the chunk-size ceiling that would push a publication over the 16384-byte reserve, and drift in the accumulator/frontier algorithm | The search itself already establishes the property - keep `publicationTransactionBytes(chosen) <= reserveTarget` and `publicationTransactionBytes(chosen+1) > reserveTarget` as the acceptance criterion and drop the transcribed numbers; make the frontier/sibling digests a generated fixture with --check shared with the Aiken twin |
| B | `demo/midgard-validation/tests/data-breadth-boundary.test.ts` | L622-L674 two hand-transcribed tables of six numbers per Data kind compared at L744/L1182; L809 and L1484 pin maximumSourceSpan <= 132 beside the derived MIDGARD_CEK_DATA_TRAVERSE_MAX_SOURCE_SPAN bound. Fee-parameter, CML or encoder changes re-measure all twelve rows; what they catch is a silently shrunk collection that the relative accepted<=max<adjacent bounds would still admit | exactBreadthMemory (L175) shows the pattern that works - derive the expectation in the test from the shape rather than transcribe it; the accepted/adjacent byte counts could likewise be derived from the fee formula and the measured framing, or moved into a CI-run generator with --check |
| B | `demo/midgard-fault-proofs/tests/submit-init-emulator-fabricated-withdrawal.test.ts` | L88-89 MM_WITHDRAWALS_ROOT and L309 pin the counted root that buildCountedRoot produces - a root-domain or encoding change fails this without a contract violation. It does catch a silent change to the withdrawals root convention. | Derive MM_WITHDRAWALS_ROOT at test time from buildCountedRoot (it already is; L130-135) and drop the literal; or make it an external vector agreed with the node committer. |
| B | `demo/midgard-sdk/tests/tx-order.test.ts` | L515-517;L528-529;L566-569;L599;L742 pin exact datum byte counts and min-Ada lovelace values that must be hand-edited whenever the datum shape or the min-Ada formula changes; a behavior-preserving datum-field rename with a different encoded length would fail them. They do catch a silent datum growth that would push a publication past the L1 size or min-Ada budget — which is the point of the toBeLessThan(minSupportedL1MaxTxBytes) legs at L442;L475. | Keep the budget assertions (measured size < consensus limit) as the gate and derive the min-Ada expectation from the ledger formula and coinsPerUtxoByte at test time; the exact byte-count literals then become redundant and can go. |
| B | `demo/midgard-validation/tests/ordered-collection-redeemer-boundary.test.ts` | Brittle: MAXIMUM_REDEEMER_* (L73-76) are measurements over lucid/CML plus the always-succeeds blueprint's compiled code, so a blueprint rebuild or dependency bump moves them. Assurance: catches a shrunk redeemer corpus and TS/Aiken fold divergence; the script is a real compiled PlutusV3 validator, not a stub. | Pin the signed tx hex; or let the CI-run ordered-collection-boundary-aiken generator own the four constants. |
| B | `demo/midgard-watcher/tests/runtime/user-event-runtime.test.ts` | L324/L340/L406 pin `toHaveLength(2)` native captures per block and L389-392 hard-code the 1/64/65/127/128/129 wave boundaries; a legitimate change to the acquisition batch window or capture count fails these with no contract violation.
